Estimated mean surfaces by different CovNet models in the Legendre basis example with different choices of
I
. The empirical mean surfaces are also shown. The relative estimation errors (in %) are shown at the bottom of every figure. The plots are heatmaps of the 2D mean surfaces.

C.3
Computing cost

We end this section with details on implementing the method for measurements on a grid and related computing costs. Recall the generic version of a CovNet kernel (
3.1
)

\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v}),\qquad\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v}\in\mathcal{Q}.

During fitting the CovNet model, using the alternate formulation (
3.2
)–(
3.3
), we need to form the
N\times D
matrix
\mathbf{X}^{\rm NN}=(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\rm NN}(\text{\bf u}_{i}))_{n,i}
, where
\text{\bf u}_{1},\ldots,\text{\bf u}_{D}
are the grid points (see the discussion after Remark
5
). This can be done as follows:

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathrm{U}}_{D\times d}\mapsto\underbrace{\mathrm{Z}}_{D\times R}=[\underbrace{g_{1}(\mathrm{U})}_{D\times 1},\ldots,\underbrace{g_{R}(\mathrm{U})}_{D\times 1}],\quad\mathbf{X}^{\rm NN}=\Xi\,\mathrm{Z}^{\top},

where
\mathrm{U}=(\text{\bf u}_{1}^{\top},\ldots,\text{\bf u}_{D}^{\top})^{\top}
is the matrix with
\text{\bf u}_{i}
in its
i
-th row,
g_{r}(\mathrm{U})=(g_{r}(\text{\bf u}_{1}),\ldots,g_{r}(\text{\bf u}_{D}))^{\top}
, i.e.,
g_{r}
is applied row-wise for a matrix, and
\Xi=(\xi_{n,r})
is the matrix of coefficients. Also, to compute the eigendecomposition of the fitted CovNet, we need to find the matrix
\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}=(\widetilde{g}(r,s))
with
\widetilde{g}(r,s)=\int_{\mathcal{Q}}g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf u})\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf u}
. As shown in Section
4
, we approximate

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}\simeq\bigg(\frac{1}{M}\sum_{j=1}^{M}g_{r}(\text{\bf u}_{j})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf u}_{j})\bigg)_{r,s},

where
\text{\bf u}_{1},\ldots,\text{\bf u}_{M}
are i.i.d. uniformly distributed over
\mathcal{Q}
. Now, with the conventions above, the approximation to
\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}
can be obtained as:

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathrm{U}}_{M\times d}\mapsto\mathrm{Z}=\underbrace{[g_{1}(\mathrm{U}),\ldots,g_{R}(\mathrm{U})]}_{M\times R},\quad\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}=M^{-1}\mathrm{Z}^{\top}\mathrm{Z},

where
\mathrm{U}=(\text{\bf u}_{1}^{\top},\ldots,\text{\bf u}_{M}^{\top})^{\top}
and
g_{r}(\mathrm{U})=(g_{r}(\text{\bf u}_{1}),\ldots,g_{r}(\text{\bf u}_{M}))^{\top}
. Thus, in all practical implementations, the computational cost depends crucially on the cost associated with the evaluation
\mathrm{U}\mapsto[g_{1}(\mathrm{U}),\ldots,g_{R}(\mathrm{U})]
for a matrix
\mathrm{U}
.

Recall that, for the shallow CovNet model,
g_{r}(\text{\bf u})=\sigma(\mathbf{w}_{r}^{\top}\text{\bf u}+b_{r})
for
r=1,\ldots,R
. Writing
\mathrm{W}=(\mathbf{w}_{1}^{\top},\ldots,\mathbf{w}_{R}^{\top})^{\top}
and
\mathbf{b}=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{R})^{\top}
, it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}\mapsto\mathrm{Z}=[g_{1}(\mathrm{U}),\ldots,g_{R}(\mathrm{U})]\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\mathrm{Z}=\sigma(\mathrm{U}\,\mathrm{W}^{\top}+\mathbf{b}^{\top}),

where the activation
\sigma
is applied component-wise. This amounts to a computing cost of
\mathcal{O}(DRd)
.

For the deep CovNet model,
g_{r}
is defined recursively as in (
2.2
) (the parameters depend on
r
). In this case,
g_{r}(\mathrm{U})
can be obtained from
\mathrm{U}
recursively as

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sigma(\mathrm{U}\,\mathrm{W}_{1,r}^{\top}+\mathbf{b}_{1,r}^{\top}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{l+1}

\displaystyle=\sigma(\mathrm{U}_{l}\,\mathrm{W}_{l+1,r}^{\top}+\mathbf{b}_{l+1,r}^{\top}),\quad l=1,\ldots,L-1,

\displaystyle g_{r}(\mathrm{U})

\displaystyle=\sigma(\mathrm{U}_{L}\mathbf{w}_{L+1,r}+b_{L+1,r}),

where
\mathrm{W}_{1,r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times d}
,
\mathrm{W}_{2,r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times p_{1}},\ldots,\mathrm{W}_{L,r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{L}\times p_{L-1}}
,
\mathbf{b}_{1,r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{b}_{L,r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{L}},\mathbf{w}_{L+1,r}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{L}}
,
b_{L+1,r}\in\mathbb{R}
are the parameters associated with
g_{r}
. Thus, the computing cost for the evaluation
\mathrm{U}\mapsto g_{r}(\mathrm{U})
is
\mathcal{O}\big(D\sum_{l=0}^{L}p_{l}p_{l+1}\big)
, where
p_{0}=d
and
p_{L+1}=1
. However, this needs to be done for
r=1,\ldots,R
. Thus, the total cost for computing
\mathrm{U}\mapsto[g_{1}(\mathrm{U}),\ldots,g_{R}(\mathrm{U})]
for the deep CovNet model is
\mathcal{O}\big(DR\sum_{l=0}^{L}p_{l}p_{l+1}\big)
.

Finally, consider the deepshared CovNet model, where
g_{1},\ldots,g_{R}
are defined recursively as in (
2.2
). In this case
\mathrm{Z}=[g_{1}(\mathrm{U}),\ldots,g_{R}(\mathrm{U})]
can be computed recursively as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sigma(\mathrm{U}\,\mathrm{W}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{b}_{1}^{\top}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{U}_{l+1}

\displaystyle=\sigma(\mathrm{U}_{l}\,\mathrm{W}_{l+1}^{\top}+\mathbf{b}_{l+1}^{\top}),\quad l=1,\ldots,L-1,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Z}

\displaystyle=\sigma(\mathrm{U}_{L}\,\mathrm{W}^{\top}+\mathbf{b}^{\top}),

where
\mathrm{W}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times d},\mathrm{W}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{2}\times p_{1}},\ldots,\mathrm{W}_{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{L}\times p_{L-1}}
,
\mathbf{b}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}},\ldots,\mathbf{b}_{L}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{L}}
,
\mathrm{W}=(\bm{\omega}_{1}^{\top},\ldots,\bm{\omega}_{R}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{R\times p_{L}}
, and
\mathbf{b}=(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{R})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{R}
are the parameters associated with
g_{1},\ldots,g_{R}
. Thus, the cost for the evaluation
\mathrm{U}\mapsto[g_{1}(\mathrm{U}),\ldots,g_{R}(\mathrm{U})]
in this case is
\mathcal{O}\big(D\big(\sum_{l=0}^{L-1}p_{l}p_{l+1}+Rp_{L}\big)\big)
, where
p_{0}=d
.

After obtaining
\mathrm{Z}=[g_{1}(\mathrm{U}),\ldots,g_{R}(\mathrm{U})]\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times R}
, we require an additional
\mathcal{O}(NDR)
computations to get
\mathbf{X}^{\rm NN}=\Xi\,\mathrm{Z}^{\top}
. Similarly, for
\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}=M^{-1}\mathrm{Z}^{\top}\mathrm{Z}
, an additional
\mathcal{O}(MR^{2})
computations are required after obtaining
\mathrm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{M\times R}
(in this case, all the previous complexities should be understood with
D=M
). We summarize these in Table
2
for the special case when
p_{1}=\cdots=p_{L}=R
for the deep and the deepshared CovNet models (as used in our numerical studies). Note that the complexities are always linear in the grid size
D
or
M
.

Table 2:
Computational complexities associated with different tasks for different CovNet models.

For the deep and the deepshared models, we consider the case when
p_{1}=\cdots=p_{L}=R
.

Method

Complexity

Shallow CovNet

Deep CovNet

Deepshared CovNet

Evaluation

\mathcal{O}\big(DRd\big)

\mathcal{O}\big(DR^{2}(LR+d)\big)

\mathcal{O}\big(DR(LR+d)\big)

Obtaining
\mathbf{X}^{\rm NN}

\mathcal{O}\big(DR(N+d)\big)

\mathcal{O}\big(DR(LR^{2}+dR+N)\big)

\mathcal{O}\big(DR(LR+d+N)\big)

\widetilde{\mathrm{G}}

\mathcal{O}\big(MR(R+d)\big)

\mathcal{O}\big(MR^{2}(LR+d)\big)

\mathcal{O}\big(MR(LR+d)\big)

D
Covering numbers

In this section, we derive certain covering numbers that will be instrumental in the proofs of our asymptotic results. We give a brief overview of covering numbers and derive bounds on the covering numbers of some classes of functions useful in our context. The main results of this section are Lemmas
5
,
6
and
7
, which give upper bounds on the covering numbers for the restricted classes of shallow, deep and deepshared CovNet operators, respectively. The reader can skip this section and go to the next section without loss of continuation.

We start with the definition of covering numbers for general metric spaces. More details can be found in
Anthony and Bartlett 1999
,
Györfi et al. 2002
,
Wainwright 2019
. Let
(S,\rho)
be a metric space, and let
T
be a subset of
S
. For
\epsilon>0
, a finite subset
T^{\prime}
of
T
is called an
\epsilon
-cover of
T
w.r.t. the metric
\rho
if for every
t\in T
we can find an
t^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}
such that
\rho(t,t^{\prime})\leq\epsilon
. The
\epsilon
-covering number of
T
w.r.t.
\rho
is the cardinality of the smallest
\epsilon
-cover of
T
. We denote the covering number by
\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,T,\rho)
. If no finite
\epsilon
-cover of
T
exists, then the covering number is defined to be
\infty
.

In our present context, we are interested in the covering numbers of the restricted classes of CovNet operators (
6.1
) w.r.t. the Hilbert-Schmidt norm. Because of the equivalence between an operator
\mathcal{G}
and the associated kernel
g
, we get
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}=\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}
. This shows that if we define
\mathscr{F}
to be a class of non-negative definite kernels and
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}
to be the corresponding class of integral operators, then

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2})=\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathscr{F},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}).

(D.1)

Thus, it is enough to find an upper bound on the covering numbers of the classes of CovNet kernels w.r.t. the
\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})
norm. We start by deriving a general bounding result.

Theorem 7
.

Let
G
be a class of functions from
\mathcal{Q}
to
\mathbb{R}
with
\|g\|\leq M
for every
g\in G
. For a positive real number
\lambda_{N}
and an integer
R
, define the following class of functions from
\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q}
to
\mathbb{R}
:

\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}}=\Bigg\{\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v}):g_{r}\in G,0\preceq\Lambda=(\lambda_{r,s})\preceq\lambda_{N}\mathrm{I}_{R}\Bigg\}.

Then, the
\mathcal{L}_{2}
covering number of
\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}}
is bounded as

\mathcal{N}\left(\epsilon,\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\right)\leq\left[\frac{M^{2}R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\times\left\{\frac{2e(4M^{2}R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{8MR^{2}\lambda_{N}},G,\|\cdot\|\right)\right\}^{R}\right]^{R}.

We first state and prove a few lemmas, which will be used in the proof of the theorem.

Lemma 2
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a compact set and
\mathcal{H}
be a collection of functions from
\mathcal{I}
to
\mathbb{R}
such that
\|h\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\leq M
for all
h\in\mathcal{H}
. Define
\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma}=\big\{\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}h_{i}:h_{i}\in\mathcal{H},0\leq\alpha_{i}\leq\gamma\big\}
. Then, for any
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
,

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2},\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})})\leq\left\{\left(\frac{MR\gamma}{2\epsilon_{2}}+1\right)\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{R\gamma},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\right)\right\}^{R}.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that all the covering numbers defined subsequently are finite. Fix
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
and let
N_{1}=\mathcal{N}(\epsilon_{1},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})})
,
N_{2}=\mathcal{N}(\epsilon_{2},[0,\gamma],|\cdot|)
. Thus, we can find a set
{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{1}}\subset\mathcal{H}
of cardinality
N_{1}
and a set of real numbers
A^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{2}}\subset[0,\gamma]
of cardinality
N_{2}
such that for every
h\in\mathcal{H}
and every
\alpha\in[0,\gamma]
, there exists
h^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{1}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{2}}
such that
\|h-h^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\leq\epsilon_{1}
and
|\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}|\leq\epsilon_{2}
. Now, let
\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}h_{i}
be an element of
\mathcal{E}_{R,\gamma}
. Let
h_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,h_{R}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{1}}
and
\alpha_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\alpha_{R}^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{2}}
be such that
\|h_{i}-h_{i}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\leq\epsilon_{1}
and
|\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\epsilon_{2}
for
i=1,\ldots,R
. Now,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}h_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{R}(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i}^{\prime})h_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}(h_{i}-h_{i}^{\prime})\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{R}\underbrace{|\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i}^{\prime}|}_{\leq\epsilon_{2}}\,\underbrace{\|h_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}}_{\leq M}+\sum_{i=1}^{R}\underbrace{\alpha_{i}^{\prime}}_{\leq\gamma}\,\underbrace{\|h_{i}-h_{i}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}}_{\leq\epsilon_{1}}

\displaystyle=R(\gamma\epsilon_{1}+M\epsilon_{2}).

This shows that
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}:=\big\{\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime}:\alpha_{i}^{\prime}\in A_{\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime},h_{i}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{H}}_{\epsilon_{1}}^{\prime}\big\}
is an
\mathcal{L}_{2}
-cover for
\mathcal{E}_{R,\gamma}
of size
R(\gamma\epsilon_{1}+M\epsilon_{2})
. Thus,

\mathcal{N}\big(R(\gamma\epsilon_{1}+M\epsilon_{2}),\mathcal{E}_{R,\gamma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\big)\leq|{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}|=(N_{1}N_{2})^{R}.

Note that
N_{2}=\mathcal{N}(\epsilon_{2},[0,\gamma],|\cdot|)\leq\gamma/(2\epsilon_{2})+1

(
Wainwright 2019
, Example 5.2)
, which shows that

\mathcal{N}\big(R(\gamma\epsilon_{1}+M\epsilon_{2}),\mathcal{E}_{R,\gamma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\big)\leq\left\{\left(\frac{\gamma}{2\epsilon_{2}}+1\right)\times\mathcal{N}(\epsilon_{1},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\right\}^{R}.

The proof is complete after transforming
\epsilon_{1}\mapsto\epsilon_{1}/(R\gamma)
and
\epsilon_{2}\mapsto\epsilon_{2}/(RM)
.
∎

As a corollary, taking
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon/2
, we get the following.

Corollary 1
.

With
\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma}
defined as in Lemma
2
, for any
\epsilon>0
,

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})})\leq\left\{\left(\frac{MR\gamma}{\epsilon}+1\right)\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2R\gamma},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\right)\right\}^{R}.

We will also use a modified version of Lemma 16.6 in
Györfi et al. 2002
. The proof follows straightforwardly from the proof in
Györfi et al. 2002
. We give a brief sketch of the proof here for completeness.

Lemma 3
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a compact set and
\mathcal{H}
be a class of functions from
\mathcal{I}
to
\mathbb{R}
with
\|h\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\leq M
for all
h\in\mathcal{H}
. For a positive real number
\gamma
and an integer
R
, define the class of functions

\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma}=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}\,h_{i}:h_{i}\in\mathcal{H},\sum_{i=1}^{R}|\alpha_{i}|\leq\gamma\right\}.

Then, for any
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}>0
, we have

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2},\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})})\leq\left\{\frac{e(M\gamma+2\epsilon_{2})}{\epsilon_{2}}\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\gamma},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\right)\right\}^{R}.

Proof.

Assume without loss of generality that all the covering numbers defined subsequently are finite. Let
N=\mathcal{N}(\epsilon_{1},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})})
. Then, we can find a subset
{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{1}}\subset\mathcal{H}
of cardinality
N
such that for every
h\in\mathcal{H}
, we can find
h^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{1}}
such that
\|h-h^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\leq\epsilon_{1}
. Now, let
S_{\gamma}=\{(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{R})\in\mathbb{R}^{R}:\sum_{i=1}^{R}|\alpha_{i}|\leq\gamma\}
, and
S_{\gamma,\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime}
be a finite subset of
\mathbb{R}^{R}
such that for every
(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{R})\in S_{\gamma}
we can find
(\alpha_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\alpha_{R}^{\prime})\in S_{\gamma,\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime}
with
\sum_{i=1}^{R}|\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i}^{\prime}|\leq\epsilon_{2}
. Define,
{\mathcal{E}}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime}:=\big\{\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}\,h_{i}:(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{R})\in S_{\gamma,\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime},h_{i}\in{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}_{\epsilon_{1}}\big\}
. Then, for
\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}h_{i}\in\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma}
, we can find
\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}h_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}^{\prime}h_{i}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{R}\alpha_{i}(h_{i}-h_{i}^{\prime})+\sum_{i=1}^{R}(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i}^{\prime})h_{i}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{R}|\alpha_{i}|}_{\leq\gamma}\,\underbrace{\left\|h_{i}-h_{i}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}}_{\leq\epsilon_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{R}|\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i}^{\prime}|}_{\leq\epsilon_{2}}\,\underbrace{\|h_{i}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}}_{\leq M}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\epsilon_{1}+M\epsilon_{2}.

This shows that
{\mathcal{E}}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime}
is an
\mathcal{L}_{2}
cover for
\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma}
of size
(\gamma\epsilon_{1}+M\epsilon_{2})
. Thus,

\mathcal{N}(\gamma\epsilon_{1}+M\epsilon_{2},\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})})\leq N^{R}\times|S_{\gamma,\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime}|.

Now, as shown in the proof of Lemma 16.6 in
Györfi et al. 2002
,

|S_{\gamma,\epsilon_{2}}^{\prime}|\leq\left(\frac{e(\gamma+2\epsilon_{2})}{\epsilon_{2}}\right)^{R},

which shows that

\mathcal{N}(\gamma\epsilon_{1}+M\epsilon_{2},\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})})\leq\left(\frac{e(\gamma+2\epsilon_{2})}{\epsilon_{2}}\right)^{R}\times\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon_{1},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\big)^{R}.

The proof is complete upon transforming
\epsilon_{1}\mapsto\epsilon_{1}/\gamma
and
\epsilon_{2}\mapsto\epsilon_{2}/M
.
∎

With
\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon/2
, we get the following corollary.

Corollary 2
.

With
\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma}
defined as in Lemma
3
, for any
\epsilon>0

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{E}_{R,\mathcal{H},\gamma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})})\leq\left\{\frac{2e(M\gamma+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2\gamma},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\right)\right\}^{R}.

We will also need the following result about the covering number of the space of product functions.

Lemma 4
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a compact set and
\mathcal{H}
be a collection of functions from
\mathcal{I}
to
\mathbb{R}
such that
\|h\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\leq M
for every
h\in\mathcal{H}
. Define
\mathcal{E}=\big\{e:\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{I}\to\mathbb{R}\text{ with }e(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})=h(\text{\bf u})\,h(\text{\bf v}),\text{ where }h\in\mathcal{H}\big\}
. Then,

\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\mathcal{E},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{I})}\big)\leq\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2M},\mathcal{H},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\right).

Proof.

Let
e_{1}=h_{1}\otimes h_{1}
and
e_{2}=h_{2}\otimes h_{2}
be two functions in
\mathcal{E}
. Then,

\displaystyle\|e_{1}-e_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{I})}

\displaystyle=\|h_{1}\otimes h_{1}-h_{2}\otimes h_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{I})}

\displaystyle=\|h_{1}\otimes(h_{1}-h_{2})+(h_{1}-h_{2})\otimes h_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I}\times\mathcal{I})}

\displaystyle\leq\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\big(\|h_{1}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}+\|h_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2M\|h_{1}-h_{2}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{I})}.

This shows that an
\mathcal{L}_{2}
-cover for
\mathcal{H}
of size
\epsilon
provides an
\mathcal{L}_{2}
-cover for
\mathcal{E}
of size
2M\epsilon
, proving the lemma.
∎

We now proceed to prove Theorem
7
.

Proof of Theorem
7
.

Since
\Lambda=(\lambda_{r,s})
is positive semi-definite, we can find orthonormal vectors
\mathbf{e}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{e}_{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{R}
and non-negative real numbers
\eta_{1},\ldots,\eta_{R}
such that
\Lambda=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\eta_{i}\,\mathbf{e}_{i}\,\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}
. Moreover, since
\Lambda\preceq\lambda_{N}{\rm I}_{R}
, we also get that
\eta_{i}\leq\lambda_{N}
for
i=1,\ldots,R
. Using this, we can write

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v})

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{R}\eta_{i}\,e_{i,r}\,e_{i,s}\right)g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\eta_{i}\left(\sum_{r=1}^{R}e_{i,r}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\right)\left(\sum_{s=1}^{R}e_{i,s}\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v})\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\eta_{i}\,\widetilde{g}_{i}(\text{\bf u})\,\widetilde{g}_{i}(\text{\bf v}),

where
e_{i,r}
is the
r
-th coordinate of
\mathbf{e}_{i}
and
\widetilde{g}_{i}(\text{\bf u})=\sum_{r=1}^{R}e_{i,r}g_{r}(\text{\bf u})
. Since
\mathbf{e}_{i}
’s are orthonormal,
\sum_{r=1}^{R}e_{ir}^{2}=1
for every
i=1,\ldots,R
. This shows that we can rewrite
\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}}
as

\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}}=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{R}\eta_{i}\,f_{i}(\text{\bf u})\,f_{i}(\text{\bf v}):f_{i}\in G_{R}^{(0)},0\leq\eta_{i}\leq\lambda_{N}\right\},

where

G_{R}^{(0)}=\left\{\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u}):g_{r}\in G,\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}^{2}=1\right\}.

Now, for any
f\in G_{R}^{(0)}
,

\|f\|=\left\|\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}\,g_{r}\right\|\leq\sum_{r=1}^{R}|a_{r}|\,\underbrace{\|g_{r}\|}_{\leq M}\leq M\sum_{r=1}^{R}|a_{r}|\leq\sqrt{R}M,

where we have used that
\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}^{2}=1
implies
\sum_{r=1}^{R}|a_{r}|\leq\sqrt{R}
by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. So, for
f\in G_{R}^{(0)}
,
\|f\otimes f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}=\|f\|^{2}\leq RM^{2}
. Now, using Corollary
1
,

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})})\leq\left\{\left(\frac{M^{2}R^{2}\lambda_{N}}{\epsilon}+1\right)\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2R\lambda_{N}},\big\{f\otimes f:f\in G_{R}^{(0)}\big\},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\right)\right\}^{R}.

Also, using Lemma
4
, we get

\mathcal{N}\left(\epsilon,\big\{f\otimes f:f\in G_{R}^{(0)}\big\},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\right)\leq\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{R}M},G_{R}^{(0)},\|\cdot\|\right).

Plugging this into the previous equation, we get

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})})\leq\left\{\left(\frac{M^{2}R^{2}\lambda_{N}}{\epsilon}+1\right)\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{4MR^{3/2}\lambda_{N}},G_{R}^{(0)},\|\cdot\|\right)\right\}^{R}.

(D.2)

Now,
\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}^{2}=1
implies
\sum_{r=1}^{R}|a_{r}|\leq\sqrt{R}
, and thus

G_{R}^{(0)}=\left\{\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}g_{r}:g_{r}\in G,\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}^{2}=1\right\}\subset\left\{\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}g_{r}:g_{r}\in G,\sum_{r=1}^{R}|a_{r}|\leq\sqrt{R}\right\}.

Now, using Corollary
2
, we bound the covering number of
G_{R}^{(0)}
as

\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,G_{R}^{(0)},\|\cdot\|)\leq\left\{\frac{2e(M\sqrt{R}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{R}},G,\|\cdot\|\right)\right\}^{R}.

The proof follows by plugging this into (
D.2
).
∎

We will use Theorem
7
to derive upper bounds on the covering numbers of the shallow and the deep CovNet kernel classes, respectively.

D.1
Covering number of the shallow CovNet class

Recall that the (restricted) shallow CovNet class of kernels is defined as

\mathscr{F}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh}=\left\{\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,\sigma(\mathbf{w}_{r}^{\top}\text{\bf u}+b_{r})\,\sigma(\mathbf{w}_{s}^{\top}\text{\bf v}+b_{s}):\mathbf{w}_{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},b_{r}\in\mathbb{R},0\preceq\Lambda=(\lambda_{r,s})\preceq\lambda_{N}\mathrm{I}_{R}\right\},

which has the form
\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}}
, where
G=\big\{\sigma(\mathbf{w}^{\top}\text{\bf u}+b):\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},b\in\mathbb{R}\big\}
. Since the activation function
\sigma
is sigmoidal, in particular
0\leq\sigma(t)\leq 1
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\|g\|\leq\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}
for all
g\in G
. Again, since
\sigma
is non-decreasing, the VC dimension of
G
is bounded by
d+2

(see
Györfi et al. 2002
, page 314)
. Since
\mathcal{Q}
is a compact set, the measure
\nu
defined as
\nu(A):=|\mathcal{Q}\cap A|/|\mathcal{Q}|
for
A\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, is a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. So, using Theorem 9.4 in
Györfi et al. 2002
, we get that

\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,G,\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\nu)}\big)\leq 3\bigg(\frac{2e}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\frac{3e}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg)^{d+2}.

Now,
\|f\|=\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}\,\|f\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\nu)}
implies that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,G,\|\cdot\|\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}},G,\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\nu)}\bigg)\leq 3\bigg(\frac{2e|\mathcal{Q}|}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\frac{3e|\mathcal{Q}|}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg)^{d+2}.

(D.3)

Using this in Theorem
7
(with
M=\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}
), we get that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(\epsilon,\mathscr{F}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\times\left\{\frac{2e(4|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\times\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{8\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}R^{2}\lambda_{N}},G,\|\cdot\|\right)\right\}^{R}\right]^{R}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\times\left\{\frac{2e(4|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\times 3\bigg(\frac{128e|\mathcal{Q}|^{2}R^{4}\lambda_{N}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\frac{192e|\mathcal{Q}|^{2}R^{4}\lambda_{N}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg)^{d+2}\right\}^{R}\right]^{R}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\times\left\{\frac{6e(4|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\times\bigg(\frac{256e|\mathcal{Q}|^{2}R^{4}\lambda_{N}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\log\frac{\sqrt{192e}|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{d+2}\right\}^{R}\right]^{R}

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\times\left\{\frac{6e(4|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\times\bigg(\frac{256e\times\sqrt{192e}\times(|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N})^{3}}{\epsilon^{3}}\bigg)^{d+2}\right\}^{R}\right]^{R}~(\text{using }\log(x)\leq x)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{6e\times(16\sqrt{e}\,|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{(3d+7)R^{2}+R}.

We summarize this in the following Lemma.

Lemma 5
.

For the shallow CovNet class of operators
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh}
, the
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}
-covering number is bounded as

\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)\leq\left(\frac{c_{0}\times(c_{1}|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\right)^{(3d+7)R^{2}+R},

where
c_{0}
and
c_{1}
are constants independent of all the other parameters. In particular, when
R,\lambda_{N}\to\infty
, we get

\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)=\mathcal{O}\bigg(dR^{2}\log\Big(\frac{R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg).

D.2
Covering number of the deep CovNet class

The (restricted) deep CovNet class of kernels is defined as

\mathscr{F}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\lambda_{N}}^{\rm d}=\left\{\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v}):g_{r}\in\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}},0\preceq\Lambda=(\lambda_{r,s})\preceq\lambda_{N}\mathrm{I}_{R}\right\},

which is again of the form
\mathscr{F}_{R,G,\lambda_{N}}
, where
G=\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}}
is the class of deep neural networks with depth
L
and layer-wise widths
p_{1},\ldots,p_{L}
(
2.2
).

Let
K=p_{1}+\cdots+p_{L}
be the number of
computation units
of a network from the class
\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}}
and
W=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}p_{\ell}(p_{\ell-1}+1)+p_{L}
be the number of
adjustable parameters

(see
Anthony and Bartlett 1999
, Chapter 6 for details)
. Now, by
Anthony and Bartlett 1999
, we can find constants
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
such that for any probability distribution
\nu
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\log\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\nu)})\leq\alpha_{1}\textrm{fat}_{\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}}}(\alpha_{2}\epsilon)\log^{2}\left(\frac{\textrm{fat}_{\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}}}(\alpha_{2}\epsilon^{2})}{\epsilon}\right),

where
\textrm{fat}_{\mathscr{F}}(\cdot)
is the
fat-shattering dimension
of the class of functions
\mathscr{F}

(see
Anthony and Bartlett 1999
, Chapter 11)
. Thus, using (
D.3
)

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}},\|\cdot\|)

\displaystyle=\log\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}},\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\nu)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{1}\textrm{fat}_{\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}}}\left(\alpha_{2}\frac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}}\right)\log^{2}\left(\frac{\textrm{fat}_{\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}}}(\alpha_{2}\epsilon^{2}/|\mathcal{Q}|)\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}}{\epsilon}\right).

Again, for any
\delta>0
,
\textrm{fat}_{\mathscr{F}}(\delta)\leq\textrm{Pdim}(\mathscr{F})
, where
\textrm{Pdim}(\mathscr{F})
is the
pseudo-dimension
of the class of functions
\mathscr{F}

(see
Anthony and Bartlett 1999
, Theorem 11.13(i))
. Using this in the previous equation, we get

\log\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}},\|\cdot\|)\leq\alpha\,\textrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}})\log^{2}\left(\frac{\textrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}})\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}}{\epsilon}\right),

for some positive constant
\alpha
. Finally, since the activation function is sigmoidal, using Theorem 14.2 in
Anthony and Bartlett 1999
, we get

\textrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}})\leq\big((W+2)K\big)^{2}+11(W+2)K\log_{2}\big(18(W+2)K^{2}\big)=:d_{W,K}.

Now, Theorem
7
gives us that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\epsilon,\mathscr{F}^{\rm d}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\lambda_{N}},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})})

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq R\left[\log\bigg(\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\bigg)+R\left\{\log\bigg(\frac{2e(4|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\bigg)+\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{8\sqrt{|\mathcal{Q}|}R^{2}\lambda_{N}},\mathcal{D}_{L,\mathbf{p}},\|\cdot\|\bigg)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq R\left[\log\bigg(\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\bigg)+R\left\{\log\bigg(\frac{2e(4|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon)}{\epsilon}\bigg)+\alpha\,d_{W,K}\log^{2}\bigg(\frac{8|\mathcal{Q}|d_{W,K}R^{2}\lambda_{N}}{\epsilon}\bigg)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq R\Big\{1+R\big(1+\alpha\,d_{W,K}\big)\Big\}\log^{2}\bigg(\frac{c_{0}|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\bigg)

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt=\Big\{\big(1+\alpha\,d_{W,K}\big)R^{2}+R\Big\}\log^{2}\bigg(\frac{c_{0}d_{W,K}|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\bigg),

for some constant
c_{0}
and
\alpha
. We summarize it in the following lemma.

Lemma 6
.

Consider the deep CovNet class of operators
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm d}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma}
, where
L
is the number of hidden layers and
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{L})
are the number of nodes at each of the hidden layers,
\sigma
is the sigmoidal activation function, and
R
is the number of components of the model. Then, there exist constants
\alpha
and
c_{0}
such that

\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm d}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)\leq\big\{(1+\alpha\,d_{W,K})R^{2}+R\big\}\,\log^{2}\bigg(\frac{c_{0}d_{W,K}|\mathcal{Q}|R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\bigg),

where
W=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}p_{\ell}(p_{\ell-1}+1)+p_{L}
,
K=\sum_{\ell=1}^{L}p_{\ell}
, and
d_{W,K}=\big((W+2)K\big)^{2}+11(W+2)K\log_{2}\big(18(W+2)K^{2}\big)
.

Remark 12
.

If we define
p_{\rm max}=\max\{d,p_{1},\ldots,p_{L}\}
, then
W=\mathcal{O}(Lp_{\rm max}^{2})
,
K=\mathcal{O}(Lp_{\rm max})
, and hence
d_{W,K}=\mathcal{O}(L^{4}p_{\max}^{6})
. This shows that

\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm d}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)=\mathcal{O}\bigg(L^{4}R^{2}p_{\rm max}^{6}\,\log^{2}\Big(\frac{L^{4}R^{2}\lambda_{N}p_{\rm max}^{6}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg).

In particular, when
p_{1},\ldots,p_{L}=R
and
R>d
, we get that

\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm d}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)=\mathcal{O}\bigg(L^{4}R^{8}\,\log^{2}\Big(\frac{L^{4}R^{8}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg).

D.3
Covering number of the deepshared CovNet class

Although the deepshared CovNet kernel has a similar structure to the deep CovNet kernel, unfortunately, Theorem
7
is not useful to bound the covering number of the deepshared CovNet class of operators. This is due to the fact that the shared structure of the constituents
g_{1},\ldots,g_{R}
of a deepshared CovNet kernel
\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v})
cannot be catered for using Theorem
7
. So, here, we proceed in a different way.

First, consider a kernel
g
from the deepshared CovNet class of kernels
\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma}
(see (
2.2
)). We have demonstrated that the kernel
g
is a neural network (see Figure
3
). For constants
\theta
and
\mu
, we define another kernel
g^{\prime}
associated to
g
as follows:

g^{\prime}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})=\textrm{{1}}\{\theta\,g(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})+\mu>0\},~~\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v}\in\mathcal{Q}.

The kernel
g^{\prime}
also has the neural network structure (see Figure
13
). It has one additional input and one additional layer than
g
, and the output has the
linear threshold
activation function. We define
\mathscr{F}^{\prime}
to be the class of all derived kernels from the deepshared CovNet kernel class
\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma}
.

Fig 13:
A schematic representation of the network
g^{\prime}
derived from a deepshared network
g
.

By construction, using Theorem 14.1 in
Anthony and Bartlett 1999
, we get that

\displaystyle\textrm{Pdim}(\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma})\leq\textrm{VCdim}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime}),

(D.4)

where
\textrm{VCdim}(\mathscr{F})
is the Vapnik-Cherbonenkis dimension of the class of functions
\mathscr{F}
. Let
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{W}
denote all the free parameters of the kernel
g^{\prime}
(i.e., the weights and biases of the different layers). It is not difficult to see that
W=\sum_{l=1}^{L}p_{l}(p_{l-1}+1)+R(p_{L}+1)+R(R+1)/2+2
, where
p_{0}=d
. Thus,
g^{\prime}
can be viewed as a function from
\mathbb{R}^{2d\times W}
to
\mathbb{R}
, which maps an element
\text{\bf w}=(\text{\bf u}^{\top},\text{\bf v}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}
and
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{W}
to
g^{\prime}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})
. Also, the function can be evaluated using
t:=4\sum_{l=1}^{L}p_{l}(p_{l-1}+1)+4R(p_{L}+1)+2R^{2}+2
basic operations of the form:

•

the exponential function
x\mapsto\exp(x)
,

•

the arithmetic operations
+,-,\times,\div
on real numbers,

•

jumps conditioned on
>,\geq,<,\leq,=,\neq
and comparisons of real numbers,

and the output is
\{0,1\}
-valued. Moreover, only
K=2\sum_{l=1}^{L}p_{l}+2R
of these operations involve the application of the exponential function. Thus, from Theorem 8.14 in
Anthony and Bartlett 1999
, we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{VCdim}(\mathscr{F}^{\prime})\leq(W(K+1))^{2}+11W(K+1)(t+\log_{2}(9W(K+1)).

(D.5)

Combining (
D.4
) and (
D.5
), we get that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma})\leq(W(K+1))^{2}+11W(K+1)(t+\log_{2}(9W(K+1))=:d_{W,K,t}.

(D.6)

Now, using the same derivations used for the deep CovNet class, we get

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\big)=\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{|\mathcal{Q}|},\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma},\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\nu)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{1}\mathrm{fat}_{\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma}}\bigg(\alpha_{2}\frac{\epsilon}{|\mathcal{Q}|}\bigg)\,\log^{2}\Bigg(\frac{\mathrm{fat}_{\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma}}\Big(\frac{\alpha_{2}\epsilon^{2}}{|\mathcal{Q}|^{2}}\Big)|\mathcal{Q}|}{\epsilon}\Bigg)~~\text{\cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{anthony1999}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Theorem~18.8)}}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{1}\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma})\,\log^{2}\bigg(\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|\,\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathscr{F}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma})}{\epsilon}\bigg)~~\text{\cite[citep]{(\@@bibref{AuthorsPhrase1Year}{anthony1999}{\@@citephrase{, }}{}, Theorem~11.13(i))}}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{1}\,d_{W,K,t}\,\log^{2}\bigg(\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|\,d_{W,K,t}}{\epsilon}\bigg).~~~~\text{(by \eqref{eq:cover_deepshared_pdim_bound2})}

We summarize this in the following lemma.

Lemma 7
.

Consider the deepshared CovNet class of operators
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma}
, where
L
is the number of hidden layers and
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{L})
are the number of nodes at each of the hidden layers,
\sigma
is the sigmoidal activation function, and
R
is the number of components of the model. Then, there exists
\alpha>0
such that

\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)\leq\alpha\,d_{W,K,t}\,\log^{2}\bigg(\frac{|\mathcal{Q}|\,d_{W,K,t}}{\epsilon}\bigg),

where
W=\sum_{l=1}^{L}p_{l}(p_{l-1}+1)+R(p_{L}+1)+R(R+1)/2+2
,
K=2\sum_{l=1}^{L}p_{l}+2R
,
t=4\sum_{l=1}^{L}p_{l}(p_{l-1}+1)+4R(p_{L}+1)+2R^{2}+2
, and
d_{W,K,t}=\big(W(K+1)\big)^{2}+11W(K+1)\,\big(t+\log_{2}(9W(K+1)\big)
.

Remark 13
.

By defining
p_{\rm max}=\max\{d,p_{1},\ldots,p_{L}\}
, we get
W=\mathcal{O}(Lp_{\rm max}^{2}+Rp_{\max}+R^{2})
,
K=\mathcal{O}(Lp_{\rm max}+R)
and
t=\mathcal{O}(Lp_{\rm max}^{2}+Rp_{\rm max}+R^{2})
. Thus,
d_{W,K,t}=\mathcal{O}(L^{4}p_{\rm max}^{6}+R^{6}+L^{2}R^{2}p_{\rm max}^{4}+L^{2}R^{4}p_{\rm max}^{2}+LR^{3}p_{\rm max}^{3})
. In particular, when
p_{1}=\ldots=p_{L}=R
and
R\geq d
, we get that
d_{W,K,t}=\mathcal{O}(L^{4}R^{6})
, and hence

\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\mathbf{p},\sigma},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)=\mathcal{O}\bigg(L^{4}R^{6}\,\log^{2}\Big(\frac{LR}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg).

E
Proofs of the asymptotic results

Here, we provide detailed proofs of the asymptotic properties of the CovNet estimators, as laid out in Section
6
. We will first derive our results for a general class of models, and then obtain the corresponding results for the CovNet structures as special cases. To be precise, consider a general class of operators
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
(depending on
N
and possibly on other parameters, which we suppress for ease of exposition) satisfying
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq\gamma_{N}
for every
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
. Define the following estimator based on
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
:

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\in\operatornamewithlimits{arg\,min}_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}-\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2},

where
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}
is the empirical covariance operator based on
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}
. We will prove two types of results for this estimator based on two
bias-variance-type
decomposition.

(A)

Consistency
: We will show that
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\leq B_{N}+2V_{N}
, where
B_{N}:=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}
is the
bias
of the estimator and
V_{N}
is the
variance
term, defined as
V_{N}=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\Big|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big|
.
We will derive conditions for convergence of
V_{N}
to
0
in terms of
N
,
\gamma_{N}
,
\beta_{N}
and
\mathcal{N}(\cdot,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2})
, the covering number of
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
w.r.t. the
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}
norm (see Appendix
D
for details). This will be used to establish weak (in probability) or strong (almost sure) convergence of the estimator.

(B)

Rate of convergence
: Here, we will show that
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\leq\widetilde{B}_{N}+\widetilde{V}_{N}
, where the bias term satisfies
\mathbb{E}\widetilde{B}_{N}=2\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
. The variance term in this case is
\widetilde{V}_{N}=\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-2\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)
. We will derive an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{V}_{N}>\epsilon)
in terms of
N
,
\gamma_{N}
,
\beta_{N}
and the covering number of
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
. This will be used to derive an upper bound for
\mathbb{E}\widetilde{V}_{N}
and subsequently, the rate of convergence.

E.1
Consistency

We start with the consistency. In what follows, we denote by
\mathscr{X}_{N}
the data at hand, i.e.,
\mathscr{X}_{N}=\{\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}\}
. Since
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}
is an unbiased estimator of
\mathcal{C}
, for any operator
\mathcal{G}
,

\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}.

(E.1)

Now, let
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}
be a random element distributed identically to
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}
and independent of
\mathscr{X}_{N}
(e.g., generated as the empirical covariance of i.i.d. observations distributed identically to
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}
, but independent of
\mathscr{X}_{N}
). Then, using (
E.1
) we obtain

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)-\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)+\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big).

Now,

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big\}=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}=B_{N},

where we have used (
E.1
). For the other part, we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)-\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}-\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\bigg|=:2V_{N},

where we have used that
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}=\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
for
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
. Using these, we get the
bias-variance
type decomposition

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\leq B_{N}+2V_{N},

(E.2)

where
B_{N}=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
and
V_{N}=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\big|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}-\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big|
. The bias term
B_{N}
converges to
0
, which follows from the universal approximation property of the different CovNet models. To control the variance term
V_{N}
, we use the following lemma which gives conditions under which it converges to
0
.

Lemma 8
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
be a class of operators with
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq\gamma_{N}
for every
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
. Let
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}\overset{{\rm i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{X}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\|\mathcal{X}\|^{2}\leq\beta_{N})=1
and
\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X})=0
. Define
V_{N}=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\big|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big|
. If for every
u>0
,

\frac{(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{N}\times\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{u}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\to 0\text{ as }N\to\infty,

then
V_{N}\overset{P}{\to}0
as
N\to\infty
. If in addition
(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}/N^{1-\delta}\to 0
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
, then
V_{N}\overset{a.s.}{\to}0
as
N\to\infty
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into two parts. First, we derive conditions under which
V_{N}-\mathbb{E}V_{N}
converges to
0
, in probability or almost surely (Corollary
3
). Then, we derive conditions under which
\mathbb{E}V_{N}
converges to
0
(Corollary
4
). We start with the following lemma.

Lemma 9
.

Consider the setup of Lemma
8
. Then, for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|V_{N}-\mathbb{E}V_{N}|>t\big)\leq 2\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt^{2}}{8\beta_{N}^{2}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}\bigg\}.

As a consequence, we get the following.

Corollary 3
.

Consider the setup of Lemma
8
.

(a)

If
\beta_{N}^{2}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}/N\to 0
, then
V_{N}-\mathbb{E}V_{N}\overset{P}{\to}0
as
N\to\infty
.

(b)

If
\beta_{N}^{2}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}/N^{1-\delta}\to 0
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
, then
V_{N}-\mathbb{E}V_{N}\overset{a.s.}{\to}0
as
N\to\infty
.

Proof.

Part (a) about convergence in probability is easy. For part (b), note that for any
t\geq 0
,

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(|V_{N}-\mathbb{E}V_{N}|\geq t\big)\leq 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt^{2}}{8\beta_{N}^{2}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}\bigg\}=2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\exp\bigg\{-N^{\delta}\frac{N^{1-\delta}t^{2}}{8\beta_{N}^{2}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}\bigg\}<\infty.

The result now follows from the Borel-Cantelli lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
9
.

For any
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
,

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{X}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{m}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}.

(E.3)

For
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
, define
h_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})\times\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})\to\mathbb{R}
as

h_{\mathcal{G}}(x,y)={\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-x\otimes x,\mathcal{G}-y\otimes y\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2},~x,y\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}).

Then, for any
x,y\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})
with
\|x\|^{2},\|y\|^{2}\leq\beta_{N}
, we get

\displaystyle\big|h_{\mathcal{G}}(x,x)-h_{\mathcal{G}}(y,y)\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-x\otimes x|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-y\otimes y|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big|=\Big|{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle y\otimes y-x\otimes x,2\mathcal{G}-x\otimes x-y\otimes y\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\big(\|x\|^{2}+\|y\|^{2}\big)\,\big(2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}+\|x\|^{2}+\|y\|^{2}\Big)\leq 4\beta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N}).

Also, for any
x,y,z\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})
with
\|x\|^{2},\|y\|^{2},\|z\|^{2}\leq\beta_{N}

\displaystyle\big|h_{\mathcal{G}}(x,z)-h_{\mathcal{G}}(y,z)\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-x\otimes x,\mathcal{G}-z\otimes z\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}-{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-y\otimes y,\mathcal{G}-z\otimes z\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle y\otimes y-x\otimes x,\mathcal{G}-z\otimes z\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\Big|\leq\big(\|x\|^{2}+\|y\|^{2}\big)\,\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}+\|z\|^{2}\big)\leq 2\beta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N}).

Now, for
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
, define
f_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})^{N}\to\mathbb{R}
as
f_{\mathcal{G}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N})=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(x_{n},x_{m})
. The function
f_{\mathcal{G}}
is symmetric in its arguments, and for any
x,x^{\prime},x_{2},\ldots,x_{N}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})
with squared norm bounded by
\beta_{N}
,

\big|f_{\mathcal{G}}(x,x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})-f_{\mathcal{G}}(x^{\prime},x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})\big|\leq 4N\beta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N}).

Then, by defining
g:\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})^{N}\to\mathbb{R}
as

g(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N})=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\Big|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\big\{h_{\mathcal{G}}(x_{n},x_{m})-\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})\big\}\Big|=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\Big|f_{\mathcal{G}}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N})-\mathbb{E}f_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N})\Big|,

we get that
g
is a symmetric function in its arguments, and for any
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
,

\displaystyle\Big|f_{\mathcal{G}}(x,x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})-\mathbb{E}f_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N})\Big|-g(x^{\prime},x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})

\displaystyle\leq\Big|f_{\mathcal{G}}(x,x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})-\mathbb{E}f_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N})\Big|-\Big|f_{\mathcal{G}}(x^{\prime},x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})-\mathbb{E}f_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|f_{\mathcal{G}}(x,x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})-f_{\mathcal{G}}(x^{\prime},x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})\Big|\leq 4N\beta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N}).

This, upon taking supremum over
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
and interchanging the roles of
x,x^{\prime}
, gives us

|g(x,x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})-g(x^{\prime},x_{2},\ldots,x_{N})|\leq 4N\beta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N}).

Now, using the method of bounded difference
(
Wainwright 2019
, Corollary 2.21)
,
we get that, for all
t\geq 0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|g(\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N})-\mathbb{E}g(\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N})|>t\big)\leq 2\exp\bigg\{-\frac{t^{2}}{8N^{3}\beta_{N}^{2}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}\bigg\}.

The proof is complete upon noting that
V_{N}=g(\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N})/N^{2}
(cf. (
E.3
)).
∎

Next, we derive bounds on
\mathbb{E}V_{N}
.

Lemma 10
.

Consider the setup of Lemma
8
. Then, for every
u>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}V_{N}\leq 4u

\displaystyle+\frac{16(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}{N^{3}u}\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{Nu}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{N^{3}u^{2}}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+\frac{128(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{Nu}\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{u}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nu^{2}}{32(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}.

This gives us the following condition for the convergence of
\mathbb{E}V_{N}
to
0
.

Corollary 4
.

Consider the setup of Lemma
8
. If for every
u>0
,

\frac{(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{N}\times\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{u}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\to 0\text{ as }N\to\infty,

then
\mathbb{E}V_{N}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
.

Proof.

The condition ensures that for every
u>0
,
\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}V_{N}\leq 4u
. The proof is completed upon taking limit as
u
goes to
0
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
10
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}^{\prime}\overset{{\rm i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{X}
be independent of
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}
. Now,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}V_{N}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{m}^{\prime})\bigg|\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{m}^{\prime})\bigg|\,\Big|\mathcal{D}_{N}\Bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{m}^{\prime})\bigg|\,\Big|\mathcal{D}_{N}\Bigg\}\Bigg]~~(\text{by Fatou's lemma})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{m}^{\prime})\bigg|\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Big\{h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})-h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})\Big\}\bigg|+\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\sumsum_{1\leq n\neq m\leq N}\Big\{h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})-h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime},\mathcal{X}_{m}^{\prime})\Big\}\bigg|\Bigg]

\displaystyle=:E_{1}+E_{2}.

(E.4)

By symmetry, we can show that

\displaystyle E_{1}\leq\frac{2}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\bigg]\text{ and }E_{2}\leq\frac{2}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\sumsum_{1\leq n\neq m\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})\bigg|\bigg],

(E.5)

where
\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{N}
are i.i.d.
Rademacher
random variables, which take the values
\pm 1
with equal probability, independent of all the other variables. Next, we derive upper bounds on

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\bigg]\text{ and }\frac{2}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\sumsum_{1\leq n\neq m\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})\bigg|\bigg].

For any
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}\big\{h_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})-h_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\big\}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{2}-2\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\times\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{2}-2\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})\times{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}.

Now, for
\delta>0
, let
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}(\delta)
be an
\delta
-cover of the least possible size for
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
w.r.t. the
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}
norm. Then, using the previous equation, for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>t\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\,\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>t\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\,\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\big(\frac{t}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})}\big)}\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>\frac{t}{2}\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\Big(\frac{t}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})}\Big)\Big|\times\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\big(\frac{t}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})}\big)}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>\frac{t}{2}\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg\}\Bigg].

Note that the cardinality of
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}(\delta)
is
\mathcal{N}(\delta,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2})
. Also,
\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\leq N(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}
for all
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}(t/(4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})))
. So, using the Bernstein’s inequality we get that for all
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}(t/(4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})))
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>\frac{t}{2}\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg\}\leq 2\,\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt^{2}}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}\bigg\},

where the bound is free of
\mathscr{X}_{N}
. All these give us that for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>t\Bigg\}\leq 2\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{t}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt^{2}}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}\bigg\}.

(E.6)

Again, for any
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sumsum_{1\leq m\neq n\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}\big\{h_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})-h_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})\big\}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sumsum_{1\leq m\neq n\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{2}-\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}-\mathcal{X}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{m}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\times\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sumsum_{1\leq n\neq m\leq N}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{2}-\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}-\mathcal{X}_{m}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{m}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\,(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})\,{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}.

Using this, and proceeding in a similar way as before, we can show that for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sumsum_{1\leq m\neq n\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{m},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>t\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{t}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\bigg)\times\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\big(\frac{t}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})}\big)}\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sumsum_{1\leq m\neq n\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{m},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>\frac{t}{2}\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg\}\Bigg].

Note that
|h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})|\leq(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}
almost surely for all
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
. Thus, using the method of bounded difference
(
Wainwright 2019
, Corollary 2.21)
, we can show that for all
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}(t/(4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})))
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sumsum_{1\leq m\neq n\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{m},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>\frac{t}{2}\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg\}\leq 2\,\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt^{2}}{32(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\},

for all
t>0
. Thus, we finally get that for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sumsum_{1\leq n\neq m\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})\bigg|>t\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 129.16626pt\leq 2\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{t}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt^{2}}{32(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}.

(E.7)

Now, for a non-negative random variable
Y
,

\mathbb{E}(Y)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(Y>t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq u+\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(Y>t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

holds for every
u>0
. Using this with (
E.6
), we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|>t\Bigg]

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq u+2\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{Nt}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{N^{3}t^{2}}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq u+2\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{Nu}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\int_{u}^{\infty}\exp\bigg\{-\frac{N^{3}ut}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt=u+2\times\frac{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}}{N^{3}u}\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{Nu}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{N^{3}u^{2}}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}.

Similarly, from (
E.1
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg|\sumsum_{1\leq n\neq m\leq N}\zeta_{n}\zeta_{m}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{X}_{m})\bigg|>t\Bigg]

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq u+2\times\frac{32(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{Nu}\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{u}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nu^{2}}{32(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}.

The proof is complete upon substituting these upper bounds in (
E.1
) in addition to (
E.5
).
∎

The proof of Lemma
8
now follows upon noting that the stipulated assumptions imply that the conditions of Corollaries
3
and
4
are satisfied.
∎

Next, we derive an upper bound on the Hilbert-Schmidt norm of an operator from the shallow, deep and deepshared CovNet classes. In particular, we get the following.

Proposition 2
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
be any of the three CovNet classes of operators (i.e., shallow/deepshared/deep). Then, for any
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq R\lambda_{N}|\mathcal{Q}|
.

Proof.

We start with the class of shallow CovNet operators
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh}
. Let
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh}
be an operator with kernel of the form

g(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,\sigma(\mathbf{w}_{r}^{\top}\text{\bf u}+b_{r})\,\sigma(\mathbf{w}_{s}^{\top}\text{\bf v}+b_{s}),\quad\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v}\in\mathcal{Q},

where
\Lambda=(\lambda_{r,s})
satisfies
\mathrm{0}\preceq\Lambda\preceq\lambda_{N}\mathrm{I}_{R}
. Since
g
is a non-negative definite kernel

\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}:=\sup_{\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v}\in\mathcal{Q}}|g(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})|=\sup_{\text{\bf u}\in\mathcal{Q}}g(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf u}).

It is easy to see that for any
\text{\bf u}\in\mathcal{Q}
,

g(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf u})=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,\sigma(\mathbf{w}_{r}^{\top}\text{\bf u}+b_{r})\,\sigma(\mathbf{w}_{s}^{\top}\text{\bf u}+b_{s})=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,a_{r}\,a_{s}={\bf a}^{\top}\Lambda{\bf a},

where
a_{r}=\sigma(\mathbf{w}_{r}^{\top}\text{\bf u}+b_{r}),r=1,\ldots,R
, and
{\bf a}=(a_{1},\ldots,a_{R})^{\top}
. Since
0\preceq\Lambda\preceq\lambda_{N}{\rm I}_{R}
,
{\bf a}^{\top}\Lambda{\bf a}\leq\lambda_{N}{\bf a}^{\top}{\bf a}=\lambda_{N}\sum_{r=1}^{R}a_{r}^{2}
. Also, since
\sigma
is a sigmoidal activation function,
0\leq a_{r}\leq 1
for
r=1,\ldots,R
. This gives us
\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\leq R\lambda_{N}
, which in turn shows

\|g\|^{2}_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}=\iint_{\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q}}g^{2}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf u}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf v}\leq R^{2}\lambda_{N}^{2}|\mathcal{Q}|^{2}.

Thus, for every
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh}
,
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}=\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\leq R\lambda_{N}|\mathcal{Q}|
, proving the result for the shallow CovNet class. For the deep and the deepshared CovNet classes, consider a kernel of the form

g(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v}),\quad\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v}\in\mathcal{Q},

where
\|g_{r}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\infty}(\mathcal{Q})}\leq M
for all
r=1,\ldots,R
, and
\mathrm{0}\preceq\Lambda=(\lambda_{r,s})\preceq\lambda_{N}\,\mathrm{I}_{R}
. Then, similarly to the previous derivations, it can be shown that
\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{\infty}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\leq R\lambda_{N}M^{2}
and
\|g\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}\times\mathcal{Q})}\leq R\lambda_{N}M^{2}|\mathcal{Q}|
. Since our activation function is sigmoidal, in particular since
|\sigma(t)|\leq 1
for all
t
, it follows that
M\leq 1
. Thus, for the deep as well as the deepshared CovNet class, we get that
\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq R\lambda_{N}|\mathcal{Q}|
.
∎

Now, we combine the pieces together. For the shallow CovNet estimator, using (
E.2
), we get that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{\rm sh}_{R,N}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\leq B_{N}+2V_{N},

where
B_{N}=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
and
V_{N}=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh}}\big|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big|
. By Theorem
1
and Remark
11
,
B_{N}\to 0
as
R,\lambda_{N}\to\infty
. From Lemma
8
, we know that
V_{N}\overset{P}{\to}0
if, for all
u>0
,

\frac{(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{N}\times\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{u}{4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\to 0\text{ as }N\to\infty,

and
V_{N}\overset{a.s.}{\to}0
if additionally
(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}/N^{1-\delta}\to 0
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
, where
\gamma_{N}=\operatornamewithlimits{arg\,max}_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm sh}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}
. By Proposition
2
,
\gamma_{N}\leq R\lambda_{N}|\mathcal{Q}|
. Also, Lemma
5
shows that

\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm sh}_{R,\lambda_{N}},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)=\mathcal{O}\bigg(dR^{2}\log\Big(\frac{R^{2}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg),~~\text{ as }R,\lambda_{N}\to\infty.

Thus, when
R,\lambda_{N}\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
in such a way that
dR^{2}\Delta_{N}^{4}\log(\Delta_{N})/N\to 0
, where
\Delta_{N}=\max\{\beta_{N},\gamma_{N}\}=\max\{\beta_{N},|\mathcal{Q}|R\lambda_{N}\}
, it is easy to see that the condition for convergence of
V_{N}
to
0
in probability is satisfied. This shows the convergence of
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}^{\rm sh}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
to
0
in probability as
N\to\infty
. For almost sure convergence, we additionally require
(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}/N^{1-\delta}\to 0
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
, or equivalently
\Delta_{N}^{4}/N^{1-\delta}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
. This proves part (A) of Theorem
3
.

The proof of parts (B) and (C) follow similarly upon noting that by Proposition
2
,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq R\lambda_{N}|\mathcal{Q}|
for all
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,L,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm d}
or
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,L,\lambda_{N}}^{\rm ds}
, and the following about the covering numbers:

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm d}_{R,L,\lambda_{N}},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\bigg(L^{4}R^{8}\,\log^{2}\Big(\frac{L^{4}R^{8}\lambda_{N}+\epsilon}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)\quad\text{(Lemma~\ref{lemma:cover_covnet_deep} and Remark~\ref{remark:cover_deep_order}), and }

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}\big(\epsilon,\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}^{\rm ds}_{R,L,\lambda_{N}},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\bigg(L^{4}R^{6}\,\log^{2}\Big(\frac{LR}{\epsilon}\Big)\bigg)\quad\text{(Lemma~\ref{lemma:cover_covnet_deepshared} and Remark~\ref{remark:cover_deepshared_order})}.

E.2
Rates of convergence

To derive the rate of convergence, we use a different bias-variance type decomposition. Recall that
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}
is a random element, distributed identically to
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}
independently of the data
\mathscr{X}_{N}
. Using (
E.1
), we get

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-2\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\kern 198.05493pt+2\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{V}_{N}+\widetilde{B}_{N}.

(E.8)

For the second component, we can write
