\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{N}=2\bigg(\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\bigg)=2\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big),

so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{B}_{N}

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\bigg\}=2\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2},

(E.9)

where in the last step we have used (
E.1
). This is the bias of the estimator which, according to the universal approximation theorem, converges to
0
.

Next, we focus on the variance term
\widetilde{V}_{N}
. For
t>0
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\widetilde{V}_{N}>t\big)=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)-\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-2\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)>t\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-2\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)>t\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-2\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)>t\bigg\}~~(\text{since }\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}\eqdist\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N})

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt=\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\kern 215.2771pt>\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{t}{2}+\frac{t}{2}+\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\bigg)\bigg\}.

(E.10)

To bound the last probability, we will use the following lemma, which is a modified version of Theorem 11.4 in
Györfi et al. 2002
.

Lemma 11
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{X}\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})
be such that
\mathbb{P}(\|\mathcal{X}\|^{2}\leq\beta)=1
,
\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X})=0
and
\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{C}
. Define
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}
to be the empirical covariance operator based on
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}
be a class of operators satisfying
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq\gamma
for all
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}
. Then, for every
a,b>0
and
\delta\in\big(0,\frac{1}{2}\big]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}:\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\kern 215.2771pt>\delta\bigg\{a+b+\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 14\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon b}{80(\beta+\gamma)^{3}},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{214(\beta+\gamma)^{4}\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}.

Proof.

Note that for any
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
,

\displaystyle{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle={|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C},\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-2{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C},\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big\{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\big\}.

Thus, the probability in question equals

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big\{{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\big\}

\displaystyle\kern 215.2771pt>\delta\bigg\{a+b+\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\bigg\}\Bigg],

and we will derive an upper bound for this. For
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
, define
h_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q})\to\mathbb{R}
by

h_{\mathcal{G}}(x)={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-x\otimes x\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}-x\otimes x\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2},~~x\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}).

With this, the probability in question can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{i})\geq\epsilon\big(a+b+\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\big)\Big\}.

We first note a few facts about the function
h_{\mathcal{G}}
. Note that

-{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}-x\otimes x\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq h_{\mathcal{G}}(x)\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-x\otimes x\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2},\text{ for all }x\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}).

Thus, using
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\leq 2({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{1}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}_{2}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2})
, with
\mathbb{P}\big(\|\mathcal{X}\|^{2}\leq\beta_{N}\big)=1
,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq\beta_{N}
and
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq\gamma_{N}
, we get

|h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})|\leq\max\{4\beta_{N}^{2},2(\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\}=:\eta_{N}\text{ almost surely for all }\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}.

(E.11)

Again,

\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})=\mathbb{E}\big({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\big)={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\leq 2(\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2}).

(E.12)

Using the alternative form
h_{\mathcal{G}}(x)={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-2{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C},x\otimes x\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}
, we get

\displaystyle\textrm{Var}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\big)=4\textrm{Var}\big({\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C},\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathbb{E}\big({\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C},\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}^{2}\big)\leq 4\,{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\big(\|\mathcal{X}\|^{4}\big)\leq 4\beta_{N}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}).

(E.13)

Finally, using (
E.12
) and (
E.13
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})=\textrm{Var}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\big)+\big(\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\big)^{2}\leq(6\beta_{N}^{2}+2\gamma_{N}^{2})\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}).

(E.14)

We are now ready to prove the lemma. In our proof, we borrow heavily from the proof of Lemma 11.4 in
Györfi et al. 2002
. In what follows,
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}^{\prime}\overset{{\rm i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{X}
are distributed independently of
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}
. Also, we denote the data at hand by
\mathscr{X}_{N}
, i.e.,
\mathscr{X}_{N}:=\{\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}\}
. Define
\mathcal{G}_{N}
depending on
\mathscr{X}_{N}
such that

\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\epsilon\big(a+b+\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\big),

if such a
\mathcal{G}_{N}
exists in
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
; otherwise choose an arbitrary
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
. Then, using Chebyshev’s inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})>\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\textrm{Var}\Big\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big\}}{\Big\{\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{n}\big)\Big\}^{2}}=\frac{\textrm{Var}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)}{N\Big\{\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\Big\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\beta_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)}{N\Big\{\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\Big\}^{2}}=\frac{16\beta_{N}^{2}}{N\epsilon^{2}}\times\frac{\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)}{\Big\{(a+b)+\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\Big\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16\beta_{N}^{2}}{N\epsilon^{2}}\times\frac{1}{4(a+b)}~~\bigg(\text{using }\frac{x}{(\alpha+x)^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4\alpha}\text{ for all }x\geq 0\text{ and }\alpha\geq 0\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{4\beta_{N}^{2}}{N\epsilon^{2}(a+b)},

where, we have used (
E.13
) in the third line. Hence, for
N\geq 32\beta_{N}^{2}/(\epsilon^{2}(a+b))
,

\mathbb{P}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big\}\geq\frac{7}{8}.

(E.15)

Now,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\geq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\geq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\epsilon(a+b)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big),

\displaystyle\kern 51.6665pt\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\geq\frac{7}{8}\times\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\epsilon(a+b)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}(\mathcal{X})\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 258.33252pt(\text{conditioning on }\mathscr{X}_{N}\text{ and using \eqref{eq:lemma_covnet2_type2_general_eq1}})

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt=\frac{7}{8}\times\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\epsilon(a+b)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\bigg\}.

So, for
N\geq 32\beta_{N}^{2}/(\epsilon^{2}(a+b))
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\epsilon(a+b)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 43.05542pt\leq\frac{8}{7}\times\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\bigg\}.

(E.16)

Now, for events
E_{1},E_{2},E_{3}
,
\mathbb{P}(E_{1})\leq\mathbb{P}(E_{1}\cap E_{2}\cap E_{3})+\mathbb{P}(E_{2}^{c})+\mathbb{P}(E_{3}^{c})
, using which we write

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}),

\displaystyle\kern 51.6665pt\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})\leq\epsilon\Big(a+b+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})+\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})\Big),

\displaystyle\kern 51.6665pt\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})\leq\epsilon\Big(a+b+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})+\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})\Big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt+2\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\frac{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})}{a+b+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})+\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})}>\epsilon\bigg\}.

(E.17)

Using Theorem 11.6 of
Györfi et al. 2002
, and noting that
h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})\leq\eta_{N}^{2}
almost surely (cf. (
E.11
)), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg\{\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\frac{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})}{a+b+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})+\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})}>\epsilon\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 43.05542pt\leq 4\times\mathbb{E}\mathcal{N}_{1}\bigg(\frac{(a+b)\epsilon}{5},\{h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}:\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\},\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(a+b)N}{40\eta_{N}^{2}}\bigg\},

(E.18)

where
\mathcal{N}_{1}(\epsilon,F,\mathscr{X}_{N})
is the
random
L_{1}
-covering number
of the class of functions
F
on
\mathscr{X}_{N}
, see
Györfi et al. 2002
for details. This provides an upper bound for the second part in (
E.2
). For the first part, the second inequality inside the probability implies

\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})\geq\frac{1-\epsilon}{1+\epsilon}\times\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-\frac{\epsilon(a+b)}{1+\epsilon}.

Also,
\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X})\leq(6\beta_{N}^{2}+2\gamma_{N}^{2})\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})
(cf. (
E.14
)).
So, the first probability can be bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)

\displaystyle\kern 86.11084pt+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\times\frac{1}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})}\bigg\{\frac{(1-\epsilon)}{(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})+\frac{(1-\epsilon)}{(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-2\frac{\epsilon(a+b)}{(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})-h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big)\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(a+b)}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\kern 215.2771pt+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n}^{\prime})+h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2\times\mathbb{P}\bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(a+b)}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\kern 215.2771pt+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg].

(E.19)

Here,
\zeta_{n}
’s are
{\rm i.i.d.}
Rademacher random variables, which take values
\pm 1
with equal probability and are independent of all other variables. Next, we derive an upper bound for the probability in (
E.2
).

Given
\mathscr{X}_{N}
and
\delta>0
, let
\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N})
be the smallest subset of
\{h_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\}
such that for every
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
we can find
h\in\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N})
satisfying

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big|h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big|<\delta.

Then, for each
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
, we can find
h\in\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N})
such that

\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|=\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}\big\{h(\mathcal{X}_{n})+h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big\}\bigg|\leq\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|+\delta.

Also,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big\{h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big(h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})+h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big)\big(h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-2\,\eta_{N}\,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big|h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big|~~(\text{since }|h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})|,|h(\mathcal{X})|\leq\eta_{N})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-2\delta\eta_{N}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(a+b)}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\kern 215.2771pt+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg[\exists\,h\in\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N}):\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|+\delta\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(a+b)}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,\eta_{N}(1+\epsilon)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\kern 215.2771pt+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-2\delta\eta_{N}\bigg\}\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg[\exists\,h\in\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N}):\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(a+b)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(a+b)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}-\delta-\delta\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)\eta_{N}}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\kern 236.80481pt+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N})\big|\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N})}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(a+b)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(a+b)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}-\delta-\delta\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)\eta_{N}}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\kern 236.80481pt+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Bigg].

(E.20)

Set
\delta=\epsilon b/5
, so that

\frac{\epsilon b}{4}-\frac{\epsilon^{2}b}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}-\delta-\delta\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)\eta_{N}}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\geq 0.

With this choice, the right side of (
E.2
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{H}_{\frac{\epsilon b}{5}}(\mathscr{X}_{N})\Big|\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{\frac{\epsilon b}{5}}(\mathscr{X}_{N})}\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}a-\frac{\epsilon^{2}a}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle\kern 215.2771pt+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Bigg].

Let
V_{n}=\zeta_{n}h(\mathcal{X}_{n})
for
n=1,\ldots,N
and
\sigma^{2}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\textrm{Var}\big(V_{n}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})
. The probability on the last equation equals
\mathbb{P}\big[|N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}V_{n}|\geq C_{1}+C_{2}\sigma^{2}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big]
, with

C_{1}=\frac{\epsilon}{4}a-\frac{\epsilon^{2}a}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\text{ and }C_{2}=\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}.

Given
\mathscr{X}_{N}
,
V_{1},\ldots,V_{N}
are independent random variables with
|V_{n}|=|h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})|\leq\eta_{N}
and
\mathbb{E}(V_{n}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N})=0
for
n=1,\ldots,N
. Also, both
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are non-negative. So, using Bernstein’s inequality
(
Györfi et al. 2002
, Lemma A.2)
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{n}V_{n}\bigg|\geq C_{1}+C_{2}\sigma^{2}\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Bigg]\leq 2\times\exp\bigg\{-18N\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{(2C_{2}\eta_{N}+3)^{2}}\bigg\},

see
Györfi et al. 2002
. Plugging in the expressions for
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
and noting that

C_{1}=\frac{\epsilon}{4}a-\frac{\epsilon^{2}a}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}a-\frac{\epsilon}{32}a=\frac{7\epsilon a}{32},

we get

18N\frac{C_{1}C_{2}}{(2C_{2}\eta_{N}+3)^{2}}\geq\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{10(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}.

All these finally give us

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}a-\frac{\epsilon^{2}a}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\,\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\kern 279.86023pt\leq 2\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{10(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}.

This upper bound does not depend on
\mathscr{X}_{N}
. So, using
\mathbb{P}(A)=\mathbb{E}\,\mathbb{P}(A\,|\,B)
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\bigg|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\zeta_{n}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\bigg|\geq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a+b)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(a+b)}{4(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\kern 236.80481pt+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{8(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq 2\times\mathbb{E}\Big|\mathcal{H}_{\frac{\epsilon b}{5}}(\mathscr{X}_{N})\Big|\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{10(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}.

(E.21)

Next, we derive upper bounds on
\mathcal{N}_{1}(\epsilon,\{h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}:\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\},\mathscr{X}_{N})
and
\big|\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N})\big|
. For any
\mathcal{G}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{2}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big|h_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big|{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}_{1}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}-2{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2},\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big|{\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\kern-2.15277pt\left\langle\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{2}-2\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle\kern-2.15277pt\right\rangle}_{2}\big|

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\times\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{2}-2\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})\times{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}.

This shows that a
\delta
-cover for
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
w.r.t. the
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}
norm is equivalent to a random
L_{1}
-cover for
\{h_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\}
on
\mathscr{X}_{N}
of size
2(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})\delta
. Thus,

\big|\mathcal{H}_{\delta}(\mathscr{X}_{N})\big|\leq\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\delta}{2(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg).

(E.22)

Note that this upper bound does not depend on
\mathscr{X}_{N}
. In fact, it is not a random quantity. Again,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big|h_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big|h_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big|\,\big|h_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})+h_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta_{N}\times\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\big|h_{\mathcal{G}_{1}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})-h_{\mathcal{G}_{2}}(\mathcal{X}_{n})\big|

\displaystyle\leq 4\eta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})\times{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}_{1}-\mathcal{G}_{2}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}.

This gives us

\mathcal{N}_{1}\big(\delta,\{h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}:\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\},\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\leq\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\delta}{4\eta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg).

(E.23)

This, again, is a non-random upper bound. We now assemble all the pieces together. By (
E.2
) and (
E.2
), for every
N\geq 32\beta_{N}^{2}/(\epsilon^{2}(a+b))
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\geq\epsilon\big(a+b+\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\big)\bigg]\leq\frac{8}{7}\times(P_{1}+2P_{2}),

where, by (
E.2
) and (
E.2
),

\displaystyle P_{1}\leq 4\times\mathbb{E}\Big|\mathcal{H}_{\frac{\epsilon b}{5}}(\mathscr{X}_{N})\Big|\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{10(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\},

and by (
E.2
),

\displaystyle P_{2}\leq 4\times\mathbb{E}\mathcal{N}_{1}\bigg(\frac{(a+b)\epsilon}{5},\{h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}:\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\},\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(a+b)N}{40\eta_{N}^{2}}\bigg\}.

Using these, with the bounds on the covering numbers (
E.22
), (
E.23
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg[\exists\,\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}:\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\geq\epsilon\big(a+b+\mathbb{E}h_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{X})\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq\frac{8}{7}\times 4\times\mathbb{E}\Big|\mathcal{H}_{\frac{\epsilon b}{5}}(\mathscr{X}_{N})\Big|\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{10(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 64.58313pt+\frac{16}{7}\times 4\times\mathbb{E}\mathcal{N}_{1}\bigg(\frac{(a+b)\epsilon}{5},\{h_{\mathcal{G}}^{2}:\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}\},\mathscr{X}_{N}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(a+b)N}{40\eta_{N}^{2}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq\frac{32}{7}\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon b}{10(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{10(3\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 64.58313pt+\frac{64}{7}\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{(a+b)\epsilon}{20\eta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(a+b)N}{40\eta_{N}^{2}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq 14\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon b}{20\eta_{N}(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{40\eta_{N}^{2}\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 21.52771pt\leq 14\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon b}{80(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{3}},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{214(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\},

where we have used that
\eta_{N}:=\max\{4\beta_{N}^{2},2(\beta_{N}^{2}+\gamma_{N}^{2})\}\leq 4(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{2}
. This proves the result for
N\geq 32\beta_{N}^{2}/(\epsilon^{2}(a+b))
. For
N<32\beta_{N}^{2}/(\epsilon^{2}(a+b))
,

\displaystyle\exp\bigg\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{214(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}\,(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}\geq\frac{1}{14},

so the inequality holds trivially. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 14
.

When
\beta_{N}=\gamma_{N}
, we can mimic the same proof to get the following upper bound with slightly better constants:

14\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\epsilon b}{160\beta_{N}^{3}},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(1-\epsilon)aN}{214\beta_{N}^{4}(1+\epsilon)}\bigg\}.

We use (
E.2
) and Lemma
11
with
\epsilon=1/2,a=b=t/2
, to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\widetilde{V}_{N}>t\big)\leq 14\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{t}{320(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{3}},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt}{5136(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}.

Now, for any non-negative random variable
Y
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Y)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(Y>t)\,\mathrm{d}t\leq u+\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(Y>t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

for every
u>0
. Using this, we get that for all
u>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{V}_{N}\leq u+\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\big(\widetilde{V}_{N}>t\big)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\kern 38.74988pt\leq u+14\times\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{t}{320(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{3}},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt}{5136(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\kern 38.74988pt\leq u+14\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{u}{320(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{3}},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\int_{u}^{\infty}\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nt}{5136(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq u+\frac{14\times 5136(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{N}\times\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{u}{320(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{3}},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\times\exp\bigg\{-\frac{Nu}{5136(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}\bigg\}.

To obtain the rate of convergence of
\mathbb{E}\widetilde{V}_{N}
, we (approximately) minimize the above quantity w.r.t.
u
. In particular, by choosing

u=\frac{5136(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{N}\Bigg\{\log(14)+\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{5136(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})}{320N},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\bigg)\Bigg\},

we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widetilde{V}_{N}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5146(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{N}\Bigg\{1+\log(14)+\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{5136(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})}{320N},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\bigg)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{(\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N})^{4}}{N}\times\log\mathcal{N}\Big(\frac{\beta_{N}+\gamma_{N}}{N},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\Big)\bigg).

(E.24)

Finally, combining (
E.2
) and (
E.2
) with (
E.2
), we get the following lemma.

Lemma 12
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
be a class of operators with
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\leq\gamma_{N}
for every
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
. Let
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}\overset{{\rm i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{X}
, with
\mathbb{P}(\|\mathcal{X}\|^{2}\leq\beta_{N})=1
,
\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X})=0
and
\textrm{Var}(\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{C}
. Define
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}-\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
, where
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}
is the empirical covariance operator. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{\Delta_{N}^{4}}{N}\times\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\Delta_{N}}{N},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\bigg)\bigg),

where
\Delta_{N}=\max\{\beta_{N},\gamma_{N}\}
.

As in the case of consistency, we use this lemma in conjunction with the fact that
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq|\mathcal{Q}|R\lambda_{N}
for an CovNet operator
\mathcal{G}
from any of the three CovNet classes (shallow, deep or deepshared) and with the bounds on the covering numbers of these classes to get the rates given in Theorem
4
.

E.3
Consistency without boundedness

We now extend the consistency results by removing the boundedness condition on
\mathcal{X}
. Recall that our estimators are re-defined in this case. As before, we prove a general result and then show the particular cases of shallow, deep and deepshared CovNet models. To this effect, consider a kernel of the form

g(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\lambda_{r,s}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v}),~~\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v}\in\mathcal{Q},

(E.25)

where
\Lambda:=(\lambda_{r,s})
is positive semi-definite. We denote the corresponding integral operator by
\mathcal{G}
. For
\lambda_{N}>0
, define
\mathscr{P}_{\lambda_{N}}\mathcal{G}
to be the operator obtained by thresholding the eigenvalues of
\Lambda
to
\lambda_{N}
. That is, if
\Lambda=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\eta_{i}\,\mathbf{e}_{i}\,\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}
is the eigendecomposition of
\Lambda
, then we define
\Lambda_{\lambda_{N}}=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\max\{\eta_{i},\lambda_{N}\}\,\mathbf{e}_{i}\,\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}
to be the
\lambda_{N}
-thresholded version of
\Lambda
. We define
\mathscr{P}_{\lambda_{N}}\mathcal{G}
as the integral operator with kernel
g_{\lambda_{N}}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})=\sum_{r=1}^{R}\sum_{s=1}^{R}\widetilde{\lambda}_{r,s}\,g_{r}(\text{\bf u})\,g_{s}(\text{\bf v})
, where
\widetilde{\lambda}_{r,s}
is the
(r,s)
-th element of the thresholded matrix
\Lambda_{\lambda_{N}}
. By construction,
\mathrm{0}\preceq\Lambda_{\lambda_{N}}\preceq\lambda_{N}\,\mathrm{I}_{R}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R}
be a class of operators with kernels of the form (
E.25
) and we denote the corresponding class of restricted operators by
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}
. Define

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}-\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2},

to be the estimator without any restriction on the underlying class. Now, for a constant
\lambda_{N}>0
, our modified estimator is defined as

\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}=\mathscr{P}_{\lambda_{N}}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}.

(E.26)

The following theorem illustrates the conditions for consistency of the modified estimator. The result is similar to Theorem 10.2 of
Györfi et al. 2002
.

Theorem 8
.

Let
\mathcal{X}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N}\overset{{\rm i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{X}
, where
\mathbb{E}(\|\mathcal{X}\|^{4})<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X})=0
and
\textrm{Var}(\mathcal{X})=\mathcal{C}
. Suppose that
R_{N},\lambda_{N}\to\infty
as
N\to\infty
. Also, suppose that the underlying class of operators
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}
is an universal approximator, i.e.,
\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
. If for every
u>0
,

\frac{R_{N}^{4}\lambda_{N}^{4}}{N}\times\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{u}{R_{N}\lambda_{N}},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}},{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}\bigg)\to 0\text{ as }N\to\infty,

then
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
converges in probability to
0
as
N\to\infty
. If in addition
(R_{N}\lambda_{N})^{4}/N^{1-\delta}\to 0
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
, then the previous convergence holds almost surely.

Proof.

Note that
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)-\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)
, where
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}
is distributed identically to
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}
, independently of
\mathscr{X}_{N}
(cf. (
E.1
)). Now,
\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)=N^{-1}\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)
converges to
0
as
N\to\infty
. Thus, it is enough to show that
\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)\Big\}^{1/2}
converges to
0
as
N\to\infty
in the appropriate notion (i.e., in probability or almost surely). We write

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\kern 43.05542pt+\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=:A_{N}+B_{N}.

For the second term,

\displaystyle B_{N}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\bigg|\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\Big\{{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}~~(\text{by \eqref{eq:HS_equality_covnet1}})

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2},

which by assumption converges to
0
as
N\to\infty
. So, for convergence in probability, it is enough to show that
\mathbb{P}\big(A_{N}\leq 0\big)\to 1
as
N\to\infty
. Similarly, for almost sure convergence, it is enough to show that
\mathbb{P}\big(\limsup_{N\to\infty}A_{N}\leq 0\big)=1
.

Recall that for
\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}
,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq\gamma_{N}:=R\lambda_{N}|\mathcal{Q}|M^{2}
, where
M=\sup_{g\in\mathcal{H}}\|g\|
. Let
L>0
be arbitrary. Since
R_{N},\lambda_{N}\to\infty
, we can assume w.l.o.g. that
L\leq\gamma_{N}
. Define
\mathcal{X}_{L}
to be the projection of
\mathcal{X}
onto
\{y\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}):\|y\|^{2}\leq L\}
. Similarly, for
n=1,2,\ldots
, define
\mathcal{X}_{n,L}
to be the projection of
\mathcal{X}_{n}
onto
\{y\in\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{Q}):\|y\|^{2}\leq L\}
. We define
\mathcal{C}_{L}=\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{L})
to be the covariance operator of
\mathcal{X}_{L}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\mathcal{X}_{n,L}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n,L}
to be its empirical counterpart based on
\mathcal{X}_{1,L},\ldots,\mathcal{X}_{N,L}
. Then,

\displaystyle A_{N}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\bigg(\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\bigg(\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\kern 55.97205pt+\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\,\big|\,\mathscr{X}_{N}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\kern 55.97205pt+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}

\displaystyle\kern 55.97205pt+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}

\displaystyle\kern 55.97205pt+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}

\displaystyle\kern 55.97205pt+{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}-{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}

\displaystyle\kern 55.97205pt+\Big\{{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\kern 55.97205pt+\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\Big\}^{1/2}\bigg).

By definition, the fifth term is non-positive. Also, by construction, the third term is non-positive. Of the remaining, the first and the eighth terms are bounded above by
\big\{\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)\big\}^{1/2}
, while the fourth and the sixth terms are bounded above by
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}
. Finally, the second and the seventh terms are bounded above by
\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\Big|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}-\big\{\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)\big\}^{1/2}\Big|
. Under the assumption,
\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\big|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}-\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)\big|\to 0
in probability as
N\to\infty
(cf. Lemma
8
). Using this and the uniform continuity of
x\mapsto\sqrt{x}
on
[0,\infty)
, we get

\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\bigg|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)\Big\}^{1/2}\bigg|\overset{P}{\to}0\text{ as }N\to\infty.

For the other two terms, observe that
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}Z_{n,L}
,
where
Z_{n,L}=\mathcal{X}_{n}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n}-\mathcal{X}_{n,L}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{n,L}
. Note that
\mathbb{E}(Z_{n,L})=\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{C}_{L}
. Now,
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Z_{n,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}
, which converges almost surely to
\mathbb{E}({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Z_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2})=\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)
as
N\to\infty
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}=\mathbb{E}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}Z_{n,L}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|Z_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}+\frac{N(N-1)}{N^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathbb{E}(Z_{L})|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}+\frac{N(N-1)}{N^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{C}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}.

Since
\mathbb{E}\|\mathcal{X}\|^{4}<\infty
, this last term converges to
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{C}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
as
N\to\infty
for all
L>0
. Thus, for all
L>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(A_{N}\leq 2\mathbb{E}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}+2{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{C}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\Big)\to 1\text{ as }N\to\infty.

Note that
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{C}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\leq\mathbb{E}({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2})
. Now, using (
B.5
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{X}\otimes\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{X}_{L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big(\|\mathcal{X}\|\,\|\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\|\big)+\mathbb{E}\big(\|\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\|^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big(\|\mathcal{X}\|^{2}\big)\,\mathbb{E}\big(\|\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\|^{2}\big)\Big\}^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}\big(\|\mathcal{X}-\mathcal{X}_{L}\|^{2}\big).

By the dominated convergence theorem, the last quantity converges to
0
as
L\to\infty
. Thus, taking limit as
L\to\infty
, we get that
\mathbb{P}(A_{N}\leq 0)\to 1
as
N\to\infty
, proving the convergence of
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{R,N}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}
to
0
in probability.

For the almost sure convergence, all the steps remain the same, except now with the additional condition

\sup_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{R,\lambda_{N}}}\bigg|{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}-\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big({|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{G}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N,L}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\big)\Big\}^{1/2}\bigg|\overset{a.s.}{\to}0\text{ as }N\to\infty,

see Lemma
8
. Thus, by the same steps we get
\mathbb{P}\big(\limsup_{N\to\infty}A_{N}\leq 0)=1
, completing the proof.
∎

E.4
The case of discretely observed data

Here, we derive the asymptotic properties of our estimators in the discrete measurement regime (Section
6.2
). Again, we will derive the results for a general class of operators
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}
. We define
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{K}=(\widetilde{X}_{n}^{K}(\text{\bf u}):\text{\bf u}\in[0,1]^{d})
to be the voxel-wise continuation of the
n
-th measurement
\widetilde{\mathbf{X}}_{n}^{K}=(\widetilde{X}_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}])
, with

\widetilde{X}_{n}^{K}(\text{\bf u})=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\widetilde{X}_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]\,\textrm{{1}}\big\{\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\big\}.

Our estimator in this case is defined as

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}^{K}\in\operatornamewithlimits{arg\,min}_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}^{K}-\mathcal{G}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2},

(E.27)

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}^{K}=N^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{K}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{K}
is the empirical covariance based on
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{K},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{N}^{K}
. In the following, for an operator
\mathcal{A}
, we write
\mathbf{A}^{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{K_{1}\times\cdots\times K_{d}\times K_{1}\times\cdots\times K_{d}}
to denote its discretized version w.r.t. the voxels
V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K},1\leq i_{1}\leq K_{1},\ldots,1\leq i_{d}\leq K_{d}
, and
\mathcal{A}^{K}
to denote the voxel-wise continuation of
\mathbf{A}^{K}
. The Frobenius norm of
\mathbf{A}^{K}
is defined as

\|\mathbf{A}^{K}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{j_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}A^{2}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d};j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}].

It is easy to verify that
\|\mathbf{A}^{K}\|_{\rm F}^{2}=(K_{1}\cdots K_{d})^{2}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{A}^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}
. Thus, for a class of covariance operators
\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}
,

\operatornamewithlimits{arg\,min}_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}}\big\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{N}^{K}-\mathbf{G}^{K}\big\|_{\rm F}^{2}=\operatornamewithlimits{arg\,min}_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}^{K}-\mathcal{G}^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\approx\operatornamewithlimits{arg\,min}_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{N}^{K}-\mathcal{G}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2},

(E.28)

where the last assertion holds when
K
is large. This justifies the definition of our estimator in (
E.27
).

By (
6.4
), it follows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{K}=\mathcal{X}_{n}^{K}+\mathcal{E}_{n}^{K}
, where
\mathcal{X}_{n}^{K}=(X_{n}^{K}(\text{\bf u}):\text{\bf u}\in[0,1]^{d})
and
\mathcal{E}_{n}^{K}=(E_{n}^{K}(\text{\bf u}):\text{\bf u}\in[0,1]^{d})
are the voxel-wise continuations of
\mathbf{X}_{n}^{K}=(X_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}])
and
\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{n}^{K}=(\epsilon_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}])
, respectively, i.e.,

\displaystyle X_{n}^{K}(\text{\bf u})=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}X_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]\,\textrm{{1}}\big\{\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\big\}\,\text{ and }\,E_{n}^{K}(\text{\bf u})=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}E_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]\,\textrm{{1}}\big\{\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\big\}.

It is easy to see that
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{K},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{N}^{K}
are i.i.d. with mean zero. Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}=\mathbb{E}(\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{K}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{K})
be the covariance of
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{K}
(the existence of
\mathcal{C}^{K}
will be proved shortly). Since
\mathbf{X}_{n}^{K}
and
\bm{\mathcal{E}}_{n}^{K}
are uncorrelated, it follows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}=\mathcal{C}^{K}+\Sigma^{K}
, where
\mathcal{C}^{K}=\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X}^{K}\otimes\mathcal{X}^{K})
is the covariance of
\mathcal{X}^{K}
and
\Sigma^{K}=\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{E}^{K}\otimes\mathcal{E}^{K})
is the covariance of
\mathcal{E}^{K}
. To quantify the rate of convergence of the estimator (
E.27
), we use the following decomposition

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}^{K}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}^{K}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}+\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle={\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}^{K}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}+\mathcal{C}^{K}-\mathcal{C}+\Sigma^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}^{K}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+3{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}^{K}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+3{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Sigma^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}.

(E.29)

We will separately bound each term in the above.

Firstly, note that
\|\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}\leq 2\|\mathcal{X}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}+2\|\mathcal{E}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}
. Now,

\|\mathcal{X}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}=\int_{[0,1]^{d}}\big(X_{n}^{K}(\text{\bf u})\big)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf u}=\frac{1}{K_{1}\cdots K_{d}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\big(X_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]\big)^{2}.

Under (M1), since
\|\mathcal{X}\|_{\infty}^{2}=\sup_{\text{\bf u}\in[0,1]^{d}}X^{2}(\text{\bf u})\leq\beta_{N}
almost surely, it follows that
\|\mathcal{X}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}\leq\beta_{N}
almost surely. Under (M2), if we define
\mathcal{I}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}(\text{\bf u})=|V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}|^{-1/2}\textrm{{1}}\big\{\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\big\}
, then it follows that
X_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]=\sqrt{K_{1}\cdots K_{d}}\,\langle\mathcal{X}_{n},\mathcal{I}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\rangle
. Thus,

\|\mathcal{X}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{K_{1}\cdots K_{d}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\big(X_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]\big)^{2}=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\langle\mathcal{X},\mathcal{I}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\rangle^{2}.

Now, the functions
\mathcal{I}_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K},1\leq i_{1}\leq K_{1},\ldots,1\leq i_{d}\leq K_{d}
are orthonormal, and hence by Parseval’s inequality it follows that
\|\mathcal{X}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathcal{X}\|^{2}\leq\beta_{N}
almost surely. In a similar way, it follows that

\|\mathcal{E}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}=\frac{1}{K_{1}\cdots K_{d}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\big(E_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}]\big)^{2}\leq\beta_{N,K}^{\rm e}\text{ almost surely}.

Thus, under both measurement schemes, we get that
\|\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}\leq\widetilde{\beta}_{N,K}:=2(\beta_{N}+\beta_{N,K}^{\rm e})
almost surely. This also ensures that
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K},\mathcal{C}^{K}
and
\Sigma^{K}
are well-defined. Now, since
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{N}^{K}
is the empirical covariance based on
\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{1}^{K},\ldots,\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{N}^{K}
, which are i.i.d. with
\mathbb{E}(\mathcal{X}_{1}^{K})=0
,
\textrm{Var}(\mathcal{X}_{1}^{K})=\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}
and
\|\widetilde{\mathcal{X}}_{n}^{K}\|^{2}\leq\widetilde{\beta}_{N,K}
almost surely, it follows from Lemma
12
that

\mathbb{E}\Big({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}^{K}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\Big)\leq 2\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{N,K}^{4}}{N}\times\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{N,K}}{N},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\bigg)\bigg),

(E.30)

where
\widetilde{\Delta}_{N,K}=\max\{\widetilde{\beta}_{N,K},\gamma_{N}\}
. For the first term on the right-hand side, we use the upper-bound

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}+\mathcal{C}-\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}+\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{C}^{K}-\Sigma^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+3{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{C}-\mathcal{C}^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+3{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Sigma^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}.

(E.31)

Secondly, because of the discretized nature of
\mathcal{C}^{K}
, we get that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}^{K}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{(K_{1}\cdots K_{d})^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{j_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\iint_{V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\times V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}}\big\{c^{K}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})-c(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})\big\}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf u}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf v},

where
c^{K}
is the kernel corresponding to
\mathcal{C}^{K}
, defined as
c^{K}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})=\mathrm{Cov}\{\mathcal{X}_{1}^{K}(\text{\bf u}),\mathcal{X}_{1}^{K}(\text{\bf v})\}
. It follows that, under (M1),
c^{K}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})=c(u_{i_{1}},\ldots,u_{i_{d}};u_{j_{1}},\ldots,u_{j_{d}})
for
\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K},\text{\bf v}\in V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}
. Thus, for
\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K},\text{\bf v}\in V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}
, using the Lipschitz property of
c
, we get

\displaystyle\big|c^{K}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})-c(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{2}\|(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})-(u_{i_{1}},\ldots,u_{i_{d}};u_{j_{1}},\ldots,u_{j_{d}})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{K_{1}^{2}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{K_{d}^{2}}\bigg).

Again, under (M2),
\mathcal{X}_{1}^{K}(\text{\bf u})=\langle X,|V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}|^{-1}\textrm{{1}}\big\{\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\big\}\rangle
for
\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}
, so that

\displaystyle c^{K}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}|\,|V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}|}\langle\mathcal{C}\,\textrm{{1}}\big\{\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\big\},\big\{\text{\bf v}\in V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}\big\}\rangle_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}|\,|V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}|}\iint_{V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\times V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}}c(\text{\bf u}^{\prime},\text{\bf v}^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf u}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf v}^{\prime}.

Thus, under this scheme again, for
\text{\bf u}\in V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K},\text{\bf v}\in V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}
, we get

\displaystyle\big|c^{K}(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})-c(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{1}{|V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}|\,|V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}|}\iint_{V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\times V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}}c(\text{\bf u}^{\prime},\text{\bf v}^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf u}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf v}^{\prime}-c(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\frac{1}{|V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}|\,|V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}|}\iint_{V_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}}^{K}\times V_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}}^{K}}\underbrace{\big|c(\text{\bf u}^{\prime},\text{\bf v}^{\prime})-c(\text{\bf u},\text{\bf v})\big|}_{\leq\rho\sqrt{\frac{1}{K_{1}^{2}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{K_{d}^{2}}}}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf u}^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\text{\bf v}^{\prime}\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\rho^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{K_{1}^{2}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{K_{d}^{2}}\bigg).

Hence, under both (M1) and (M2), we get that

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\mathcal{C}^{K}-\mathcal{C}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}^{2}\leq\rho^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{K_{1}^{2}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{K_{d}^{2}}\bigg).

(E.32)

Thirdly, by the assumptions of Theorem
6
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\Sigma^{K}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(K_{1}\cdots K_{d})^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{j_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\big\{\mathrm{Cov}(E_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}],E_{n}^{K}[j_{1},\ldots,j_{d}])\big\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(K_{1}\cdots K_{d})^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{K_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}=1}^{K_{d}}\big\{\textrm{Var}(E_{n}^{K}[i_{1},\ldots,i_{d}])\big\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{K}^{4}}{K_{1}\cdots K_{d}}.

(E.33)

Combining (
E.30
)–(
E.4
) with (
E.4
), we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big({\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}^{K}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 18\inf_{\mathcal{G}\in\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\mathcal{G}-\mathcal{C}\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{2}^{2}+21\rho^{2}\bigg(\frac{1}{K_{1}^{2}}+\cdots+\frac{1}{K_{d}^{2}}\bigg)+\frac{21\sigma_{K}^{4}}{K_{1}\cdots K_{d}}

\displaystyle\kern 129.16626pt+\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{N,K}^{4}}{N}\times\log\mathcal{N}\bigg(\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}_{N,K}}{N},\widetilde{\mathscr{F}}_{N},{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\cdot|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}_{2}\bigg)\bigg),

where
\widetilde{\Delta}_{N,K}=\max\{2(\beta_{N}+\beta_{N,K}^{\rm e}),\gamma_{N}\}
. Using this, with
\gamma_{N}=R\lambda_{N}
and the derived bounds on the covering numbers, we get the rates as given in Theorem
6
.

F
Additional simulation results

In this section, we provide some additional simulation results which were skipped in the main text. We start with the estimation errors for Ex 1–5 in the main text in 2D for the fixed resolution and varying sample size regime, which are shown in Figure
14
. The results are as expected – the estimation error for all the methods decreases as the sample size increases.

\nu=0.01
)

Fig 14:
Relative errors of different methods for different examples in 2D. Results are reported for a fixed resolution of
25\times 25
and varying sample size. The numbers are averages based on
25
simulation runs.

In Ex 1 and 3, when the true covariance is separable, although all the methods perform almost similarly, the CovNet estimators seem to have an edge over the others. In Ex 2 and 4, the separability of the true covariance is broken by rotation, which has a dire consequence on the performance of the best separable estimator, but not on the CovNet estimators. The advantage of the CovNet estimators is more prominent for larger sample sizes, as can be seen in the results for the integrated Brownian motion (both the usual and the rotated versions) and the Matérn covariance models (Figures
14
(c)–(e)).

,

Fig 15:
Relative errors of different methods for different examples in 3D. In (a) and (b), results are for a fixed sample size of
500
and varying resolutions. In (c), results are for sample size
250
and resolution
15\times 15\times 15
, with varying smoothness parameter
\nu
. In (d), results are for sample size
500
and
\nu=0.01
with varying resolutions. The numbers are averages based on
20
simulation runs.

Next, we consider the results in 3D, which are reported in Figure
15
. Here, we only report the results for the non-separable examples (Ex 2, 4 and 5). For the rotated examples (Ex 2 and 4), we take
\mathrm{O}
to be the composition of the basic rotations by
45^{\circ}
along the
x,y
, and
z
-axes, respectively. Formally,
\mathrm{O}=\mathrm{O}_{z}\mathrm{O}_{y}\mathrm{O}_{x}
, where

\mathrm{O}_{x}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\
0&1/\sqrt{2}&-1/\sqrt{2}\\
0&1/\sqrt{2}&1/\sqrt{2}\end{pmatrix},\mathrm{O}_{y}=\begin{pmatrix}1/\sqrt{2}&0&1/\sqrt{2}\\
0&1&0\\
-1/\sqrt{2}&0&1/\sqrt{2}\end{pmatrix}\text{ and }\mathrm{O}_{z}=\begin{pmatrix}1/\sqrt{2}&-1/\sqrt{2}&0\\
1/\sqrt{2}&1/\sqrt{2}&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}.

The best separable estimator is designed specifically for problems which have the 2D structure (specifically, for spatio-temporal problems), with no straightforward extension to the 3D scenario. To accommodate for this, for the best separable estimator, we combined two of the three dimensions together to transform the data into 2D. This gives us three different results depending on which two dimensions are combined. In Figure
15
, we report the best (minimum error) among these three results.

In 3D, our basic findings remain the same as in 2D. The CovNet estimators outperform the empirical and the best separable estimators, especially when the resolution is low. Also, the smoothness of the integrated Brownian sheet enhances the performance of the CovNet operators, as opposed to the other two estimators. For the Matérn example, the empirical estimator performs very poorly for smaller values of
\nu
. One interesting observation here is that the effect of non-separability is not so severe on the best separable estimator, particularly for the integrated Brownian sheet. This may be due to the fact that during the construction of the best separable estimator in 3D, we merged two dimensions together, which somehow caters for the non-separability. Among the different CovNet estimators, the deepshared model had the best performance.

and

Finally, in Table
3
, we show the results for the proposed cross-validation strategy on the examples in 3D. As before, for each example, we report relative errors for the three CovNet models (shallow, deep and deepshared) with hyperparameters selected via cross-validation based on a single simulation run with
500
observations at a resolution of
15\times 15\times 15
. For each model, we also show the difference from the least observed relative error over the range of hyperparameters. The relative errors for the empirical and the best separable estimators are also reported to facilitate comparison. Similar to 2D, in all the examples, the average difference from the best result was less than
1\%
for all the CovNet models, while the maximum difference was less than
1.75\%
. So, here too, the cross-validation method identifies a good set of hyperparameters.
